In this section we present some supplementary results that have been motivated in the main body of the article.

B.1
Alternative Condition to (

H
1

)

As mentioned in the main body of the text the results also hold under a slightly modified condition (

H
1

). We denote
\|\cdot\|_{v,d_{n}}
,
\|\cdot\|_{m,d_{n}}
and
\|\cdot\|_{t,m_{n}}
respectively the
L_{1}
norm for
d_{n}
dimensional vectors,
d_{n}\times d_{n}
dimensional matrices and
d_{n}\times d_{n}\times d_{n}
dimensional tensors.
We define the following quantities:

\begin{split}R^{*}_{1,n}:=~&2n^{\frac{1}{3}}\sup_{i\leq n}\Big\|\big\|\partial_{i}f_{n}(Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}}_{1:n})\big\|_{v,d_{n}}\Big\|_{L_{12}}\Big\|\sup_{i\leq d_{n}}|X_{1,i}^{n}|\Big\|_{L_{12}}\\
R^{*}_{2,n}:=~&4n^{1/2}\sup_{i\leq n}\Big\|\big\|\partial_{i}^{2}f_{n}(Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}})\big\|_{m,d_{n}}\Big\|_{L_{12}}\Big\|\sup_{i\leq d_{n}}|X_{1,i}^{n}|\Big\|^{2}_{L_{12}}\\
R^{*}_{3,n}:=~&8n\sup_{i\leq n}\Big\|\max_{x\in[\bar{X}^{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n}]\cup[\bar{X}^{n},Z_{1}^{n}]}\big\|\partial_{i}^{3}f_{n}(Z^{n,i,x})\big\|_{t,d_{n}}\Big\|_{L_{12}}\Big\|\sup_{i\leq d_{n}}|X_{1,i}^{n}|\Big\|^{4}_{L_{12}}.\end{split}

We assume that the functions
(f_{n})
satisfy the following conditions:

\displaystyle\log(d_{n})\left((R_{n,1}^{*})^{2}\max\left\{\frac{1}{n^{1/6}},R^{*}_{n,1}\right\}+R_{n,2}^{*}\right)+R_{n,3}^{*}\rightarrow 0.

(
H_{1}^{*}
)

Theorem 13
.

Let
(g_{n}:\times_{l=1}^{n}\mathbb{R}^{d_{n}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R})
be a sequence of symmetric measurable symmetric functions. Let
(X^{n}_{i})
be a triangular array of i.i.d processes such that
X^{n}_{1}\in L_{12}
. Assume that there is a sequence
(f_{n})
of measurable functions satisfying condition (

H
0

) and (

H
1

∗

).
Then there exists a universal constant
K
such that:

\displaystyle\left\|d_{{\cal F}}\Big(g_{n}(Z^{n}),g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}\Big)\right\|_{L_{1}}\leq~

\displaystyle\Big\|g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})-f_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\Big\|_{L_{1}}+\big\|g_{n}(Z^{n})-f_{n}(Z^{n})\big\|_{L_{1}}

(77)

\displaystyle+K\left(\log(d_{n})\left((R_{n,1}^{*})^{2}\max\left\{\frac{1}{n^{1/6}},R^{*}_{n,1}\right\}+R_{n,2}^{*}\right)+R_{n,3}^{*}\right)

(78)

B.2
Extension to Exchangeable Sequences and Random Estimators
(g_{n})

In this subsection we generalize
theorem

1
to random estimators
(g_{n})
and to exchangeable processes
(X_{i}^{n})
.
We say that a process
(X_{i}^{n})
is exchangeable if and only if for all permutations
\pi\in\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{N})
and all indexes
i_{1},\dots,i_{k}\in\mathbb{N}
we have:

(X_{i_{1}}^{n},\dots,X_{i_{k}}^{n})\overset{d}{=}(X_{\pi(i_{1})}^{n},\dots,X_{\pi(i_{k})}^{n}).

We designate by
\tau(X^{n})
the tail
\sigma-
algebra of
X^{n}
which is defined as
\tau(X^{n}):=\bigcap_{i=1}^{\infty}\sigma\big(X^{n}_{k},~k\geq i\big).
By the De Finitti theorem we know that
(X_{i}^{n})
is exchangeable if and only if conditionally on
\tau(X^{n})
the process
(X_{i}^{n})
is an i.i.d process.

We assume that the sequence of (potentially random) functions
(g_{n})
is such that there is a net of (potentially random) three-times differentiable functions
(f_{n})
respecting conditions (

H
0

) and (

H
1

).
We establish under those conditions the limiting distribution of the bootstrap estimator.

Theorem 14
.

Let
(g_{n}:\times_{l=1}^{n}\mathbb{R}^{d_{n}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R})
be a sequence of measurable functions. Let
(X^{n}_{i})
be a triangular array of exchangeable processes such that
X^{n}_{1}\in L_{12}
. Assume that there is a net
(f_{n})
of (potentially random) functions satisfying
1
.
Let
Y^{n}:=(Y^{n}_{i})
be a process that is, conditionally on
\tau(X^{n})
, an independent copy of
X^{n}
that is also independent from
(g_{n})
. Define
(Z^{n})
to be a boostrap sample of
X^{n}
that is independent of
(g_{n})
conditionally on
X^{n}
. Then there exists a universal constant
K
such that:

\displaystyle\left\|d_{{\cal F}}\left(g_{n}(Z^{n}),g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}\right)\right\|_{L_{1}}\leq~

\displaystyle\left\|g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})-f_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\right\|_{L_{1}}+\left\|g_{n}(Z^{n})-f_{n}(Z^{n})\right\|_{L_{1}}

(79)

\displaystyle~+K\,\left(R_{n,1}^{2}\max\left(\frac{1}{n^{1/6}},R_{n,1}\right)+R_{n,3}+R_{n,2}\right)

(80)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow~

\displaystyle 0.

(81)

Remark 2
.

We note that
Theorem

2
,
Theorem

4
,
Theorem

7
and
Theorem

13
, can be generalized in the exact same fashion.

Appendix C
Proof of
Theorem

1
,
Theorem

13
,
Theorem

7
and
Theorem

14

As the proof of
Theorem

1
and
Theorem

13
are very similar, we present the proof for
Theorem

1
and highlight the differences with the proof of
theorem

13
. The proof of
Theorem

1
and
Theorem

14
are identical.

Throughout the proofs we will use the following notations. We write
({X^{c}_{i}})
and
(\tilde{Y}^{c}_{i})
the re-centered processes, around the empirical mean:

\displaystyle X^{c}_{i}:=~

\displaystyle X^{n}_{i}-\bar{X}^{n},

\displaystyle\tilde{Y}^{c}_{i}:=~

\displaystyle\tilde{Y}^{n}_{i}-\bar{X}^{n},

\displaystyle Y^{c}_{i}:=~

\displaystyle Y^{n}_{i}-\bar{X}^{n},

\displaystyle Z^{c}_{i}:=~

\displaystyle Z^{n}_{i}-\bar{X}^{n}.

(82)

C.1
Main Lemmas

Lemma 15

(Approximation Error)
.

Let
(f_{n})
be a sequence of
\mathbb{C}^{3}
functions that approximates
(g_{n})
as designated by
Equation

H
0

.
Then:

\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\left\|d_{{\cal F}}\left(g_{n}(Z^{n}),g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}\right)\right\|_{L_{1}}\leq\left\|d_{{\cal F}}\left(f_{n}(Z^{n}),f_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}\right)\right\|_{L_{1}}\\
+\left\|g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})-f_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\right\|_{L_{1}}+\left\|g_{n}(Z^{n})-f_{n}(Z^{n})\right\|_{L_{1}}

(83)

Proof.

Let
(f_{n})
be a sequence of
\mathbb{C}^{3}
functions that approximate approximate
(g_{n})
as designated by
1
. By Condition (

H
0

) and the fact that for all
h\in{\cal F}
,
\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}|h^{\prime}(x)|\leq 1
, we have:

\displaystyle\forall h\in{\cal F}:\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[h\left(g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\right)-h\left(f_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\right)\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}\leq~

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\left|g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})-f_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\right|\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(84)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\left\|g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})-f_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\right\|_{L_{1}}

(85)

\displaystyle\forall h\in{\cal F}:\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[h\left(g_{n}(Z^{n})\right)-h\left(f_{n}(Z^{n})\right)\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}\leq~

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[g_{n}(Z^{n})-f_{n}(Z^{n})\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(86)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\left\|g_{n}(Z^{n})-f_{n}(Z^{n})\right\|_{L_{1}}

(87)

Thus we can conclude that:

\left\|d_{{\cal F}}\left(g_{n}(Z^{n}),g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}\right)\right\|_{L_{1}}\leq\left\|d_{{\cal F}}\left(f_{n}(Z^{n}),f_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}\right)\right\|_{L_{1}}\\
+\left\|g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})-f_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\right\|_{L_{1}}+\left\|g_{n}(Z^{n})-f_{n}(Z^{n})\right\|_{L_{1}}

(88)

Therefore it is enough to study the metric distance between the distributions of
f_{n}({Z}^{n})
and
f_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})
.
∎

Lemma 16

(Lindenberg Path Decomposition)
.

For any statistic
f_{n}
and
i\in[n]
, let:

A_{i}:=\left\|\sup_{h\in{\cal F}}\mathbb{E}\left[h\left(f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i}\right)\right)-h\left(f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i-1}\right)\right)\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(89)

Then:

\left\|d_{{\cal F}}\left(f_{n}(Z^{n}),f_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}\right)\right\|_{L_{1}}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}

(90)

Proof.

By the triangle inequality and writing the difference between
h(f_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n}))
and
h(f_{n}(Z^{n}))
as a “Lindenberg” telescoping sum of interpolating differences, we have for all
h\in{\cal F}
:

\displaystyle\left\|d_{{\cal F}}\left(f_{n}(Z^{n}),f_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}\right)\right\|_{L_{1}}=~

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{h\in{\cal F}}\mathbb{E}\left[h\left(f_{n}\left(\tilde{Y}^{n}\right)\right)-h\left(f_{n}\left(Z^{n}\right)\right)\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(91)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{h\in{\cal F}}\mathbb{E}\left[h\left(f_{n}\left(Z^{n,n}\right)\right)-h\left(f_{n}\left(Z^{n,0}\right)\right)\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(92)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{h\in{\cal F}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[h\left(f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i}\right)\right)-h\left(f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i-1}\right)\right)\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(93)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\sup_{h\in{\cal F}}\mathbb{E}\left[h\left(f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i}\right)\right)-h\left(f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i-1}\right)\right)\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(94)

∎

Lemma 17

(Third-Order Approximation of Test Function)
.

For any statistic
f_{n}
, let:

\displaystyle\bar{f}^{i}_{n}:=~

\displaystyle f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}}\right)=f_{n}(\tilde{Y}_{1}^{n},\ldots,\tilde{Y}_{i-1}^{n},\bar{X}^{n},Z_{i+1}^{n},\ldots,Z_{n}^{n})

(95)

\displaystyle\Delta_{i}(f_{n},x):=~

\displaystyle f_{n}(Z^{n,i,x})-\bar{f}^{i}_{n}.

(96)

Then each quantity
A_{i}
as defined in
Lemma

16
can be bounded as:

A_{i}\leq\mathbb{Q}_{1i}+\mathbb{Q}_{2i}+\mathbb{Q}_{3i}

(97)

with:

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{1i}:=~

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}(f_{n},X_{\ell}^{n})\right\|_{L_{1}}

(98)

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{2i}:=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})^{2}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}(f_{n},X_{\ell}^{n})^{2}\right\|_{L_{1}}

(99)

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{3i}:=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\left(\left\|\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})\right\|^{3}_{L_{3}}+\left\|\Delta_{i}(f_{n},Z_{i}^{n})\right\|^{3}_{L_{3}}\right)

(100)

Proof.

By centering around
h(\bar{f}_{n}^{i})
we can re-write
A_{i}
as:

\displaystyle A_{i}=\left\|\sup_{h\in{\cal F}}\mathbb{E}\left[h\left(f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i}\right)\right)-h\left(\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right)-h\left(f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i-1}\right)\right)+h\left(\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right)\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(101)

Applying a third-order Taylor expansion of each difference around
\bar{f}_{n}^{i}
and using the fact that
h\in{\cal F}
has third order derivatives, uniformly bounded by
1
:

\displaystyle A_{i}\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{h\in{\cal F}}\mathbb{E}\left[h^{\prime}\left(\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right)\,\left(f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i}\right)-\bar{f}^{i}_{n}-\left(f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i-1}\right)-\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right)\right)\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(102)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\sup_{h\in{\cal F}}\mathbb{E}\left[h^{\prime\prime}\left(\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right)\,\left(\left(f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i}\right)-\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right)^{2}-\left(f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i-1}\right)-\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right)^{2}\right)\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{6}\left(\left\|f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i}\right)-\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right\|^{3}_{L_{3}}+\left\|f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i-1}\right)-\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right\|^{3}_{L_{3}}\right)=:\mathbb{I}_{1}+\mathbb{I}_{2}+\mathbb{I}_{3}

Bounding
\mathbb{I}_{1}
.

We now upper bound the
\mathbb{I}_{1}
term. Observe that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}_{1}:=~

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{h\in{\cal F}}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[h^{\prime}\left(\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right)\,\left(f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i}\right)-\bar{f}^{i}_{n}-\left(f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i-1}\right)-\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right)\right)\mid X^{n}\right]\right|\right\|_{L_{1}}

(103)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{h\in{\cal F}}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[h^{\prime}\left(\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right)\,\left(\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})-\Delta_{i}(f_{n},Z_{i}^{n})\right)\mid X^{n}\right]\right|\right\|_{L_{1}}

(104)

By a tower law of expectations and the fact that
|h^{\prime}(\bar{f}_{n}^{i})|\leq 1
, for all
h\in{\cal F}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}_{1}=~

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{h\in{\cal F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[h^{\prime}\left(\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right)\,\left(\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})-\Delta_{i}(f_{n},Z_{i}^{n})\right)\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(105)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{h\in{\cal F}}\mathbb{E}\left[h^{\prime}\left(\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right)\,\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})-\Delta_{i}(f_{n},Z_{i}^{n})\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(106)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{h\in{\cal F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|h^{\prime}\left(\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right)\right|\,\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})-\Delta_{i}(f_{n},Z_{i}^{n})\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right|\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(107)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})-\Delta_{i}(f_{n},Z_{i}^{n})\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right|\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(108)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})-\Delta_{i}(f_{n},Z_{i}^{n})\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(109)

Moreover, observe that conditional on
X^{n}
and
Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}}
, the only thing that varies in the random variable
\Delta_{i}(f_{n},Z_{i}^{n})
is
Z_{i}^{n}
. Moreover,
Z_{i}^{n}
is distributed uniformly over
\{X_{1}^{n},\ldots,X_{n}^{n}\}
, conditional on
Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}
(since conditional on
X^{n}
,
Z_{i}^{n}
is independent of
\tilde{Y}^{n}
):

\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta_{i}(f_{n},Z_{i}^{n})\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}(f_{n},X_{\ell}^{n})

(110)

We can then conclude that:

\mathbb{I}_{1}\leq\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}(f_{n},X_{\ell}^{n})\right\|_{L_{1}}

(111)

Bounding
\mathbb{I}_{2}
.

Observe that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}_{2}:=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|\sup_{h\in{\cal F}}\mathbb{E}\left[h^{\prime\prime}\left(\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right)\,\left(\left(f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i}\right)-\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right)^{2}-\left(f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i-1}\right)-\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right)^{2}\right)\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(112)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|\sup_{h\in{\cal F}}\mathbb{E}\left[h^{\prime\prime}\left(\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right)\,\left(\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})^{2}-\Delta_{i}(f_{n},Z_{i}^{n})^{2}\right)\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(113)

By a tower law of expectations and the fact that
|h^{\prime\prime}(\bar{f}_{n}^{i})|\leq 1
, for all
h\in{\cal F}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}_{2}=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|\sup_{h\in{\cal F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[h^{\prime\prime}\left(\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right)\,\left(\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})^{2}-\Delta_{i}(f_{n},Z_{i}^{n})^{2}\right)\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(114)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|\sup_{h\in{\cal F}}\mathbb{E}\left[h^{\prime\prime}\left(\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right)\,\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})^{2}-\Delta_{i}(f_{n},Z_{i}^{n})^{2}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(115)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|\sup_{h\in{\cal F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|h^{\prime\prime}\left(\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right)\right|\,\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})^{2}-\Delta_{i}(f_{n},Z_{i}^{n})^{2}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right|\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(116)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})^{2}-\Delta_{i}(f_{n},Z_{i}^{n})^{2}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right|\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(117)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})^{2}-\Delta_{i}(f_{n},Z_{i}^{n})^{2}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(118)

By the conditional independence reasoning we presented in the bound for
\mathbb{I}_{1}
, the latter can further be written as:

\mathbb{I}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})^{2}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}(f_{n},X_{\ell}^{n})^{2}\right\|_{L_{1}}

(119)

Bounding
\mathbb{I}_{3}
.

We simply observe that
\mathbb{I}_{3}
can be re-written as:

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}_{3}:=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\left(\left\|f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i}\right)-\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right\|^{3}_{L_{3}}+\left\|f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i-1}\right)-\bar{f}^{i}_{n}\right\|^{3}_{L_{3}}\right)

(120)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\left(\left\|\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})\right\|^{3}_{L_{3}}+\left\|\Delta_{i}(f_{n},Z_{i}^{n})\right\|^{3}_{L_{3}}\right)

(121)

∎

Lemma 18

(Third Order Approximation of Smooth Statistic)
.

Consider any statistic
f_{n}\in\mathbb{C}^{3}
and any random vector
V\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Consider the random variables:

\displaystyle\bar{{\cal J}}_{i}:=~

\displaystyle\partial_{i}f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}}\right)

(122)

\displaystyle\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}:=~

\displaystyle\partial_{i}^{2}f_{n}\left(Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}}\right)

(123)

If
\|V_{k}\|_{L_{12}}\leq\|X_{k}^{n}\|_{L_{12}}
and (

H
1

) holds, then:

\left\|\Delta_{i}(f_{n},V)-\bar{{\cal J}}_{i}^{\top}V^{c}-\frac{1}{2}\left(V^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}V^{c}\right\|_{L_{3}}\leq\frac{R_{n,3}}{6n}

(124)

If
\|\sup_{k\leq d}V_{k}\|_{L_{12}}\leq\|\sup_{k\leq d}X_{k}^{n}\|_{L_{12}}
and (

H
1

∗

) holds, then:

\left\|\Delta_{i}(f_{n},V)-\bar{{\cal J}}_{i}^{\top}V^{c}-\frac{1}{2}\left(V^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}V^{c}\right\|_{L_{3}}\leq\frac{R_{n,3}^{*}}{6n}

(125)

Proof.

Since
f_{n}
is three-times differentiable, for any random vector
V\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\|V_{k}\|_{L_{12}}\leq\|X_{k}\|_{L_{12}}
, for all
k\in[d]
, if we let
V^{c}=V-\bar{X}^{n}
, then by a Taylor expansion and
Lemma

9
:

\left\|\Delta_{i}(f_{n},V)-\bar{{\cal J}}_{i}^{\top}V^{c}-\frac{1}{2}\left(V^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}V^{c}\right\|_{L_{3}}\\
\leq\frac{1}{6}\left\|\sup_{x\in[\bar{X}^{n},V]}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\leq d_{n}}\partial^{3}_{i,k_{1:3}}f_{n}(Z^{n,i,x})V_{k_{1}}^{c}V_{k_{2}}^{c}V_{k_{3}}^{c}\right\|_{L_{3}}\leq\frac{1}{6}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\leq d_{n}}D_{k_{1:3}}^{n}(f_{n})\leq\frac{R_{n,3}}{6n}.

(126)

where we used the fact that
\|V_{k}^{c}\|_{L_{12}}\leq\|V_{k}\|_{L_{12}}+\|\bar{X}_{k}^{n}\|_{L_{12}}\leq 2\|X_{k}^{n}\|_{L_{12}}=M_{k}^{n}
. The second part of the lemma follows along identical lines, but in the second-to-last inequality we instead bound by:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\left\|\sup_{x\in[\bar{X}^{n},V]}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\leq d_{n}}\left|\partial^{3}_{i,k_{1:3}}f_{n}(Z^{n,i,x})\right|\left(\sup_{k\leq d}V_{k}^{c}\right)^{3}\right\|_{L_{3}}

(127)

By a Cauchy-Schwarz inequality the latter is upper bounded by:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\left\|\sup_{x\in[\bar{X}^{n},V]}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\leq d_{n}}\left|\partial^{3}_{i,k_{1:3}}f_{n}(Z^{n,i,x})\right|\right\|_{L_{12}}\left\|\sup_{k\leq d}V_{k}^{c}\right\|_{L_{12}}^{4}

(128)

Since
\|\sup_{k\leq d}V_{k}\|_{L_{12}}\leq\|\sup_{k\leq d}X_{k}^{n}\|_{L_{12}}
, we also have that:
\|\sup_{k\leq d}V_{k}^{c}\|_{L_{12}}\leq 2\,\|\sup_{k\leq d}X_{k}^{n}\|_{L_{12}}
. By the definition of
R_{n,3}^{*}
, we get the result.
∎

C.2
Proof of
Theorem

1

Lemma 19

(Bounding
\mathbb{Q}_{3i}
under (

H
1

))
.

For any statistic sequence
(f_{n})
, with
f_{n}\in\mathbb{C}^{3}
that satisfies (

H
1

), we have for all
i\in[n]
:

\displaystyle\max\left\{\|\Delta_{i}(f_{n},Z_{i}^{n})\|_{L_{3}}^{3},\,\|\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})\|_{L_{3}}^{3}\right\}\leq\frac{9}{n}\left(R_{n,1}^{3}+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{n}}R_{n,2}^{3}+\frac{1}{6n^{2}}R_{n,3}^{3}\right)

(129)

Therefore:

\mathbb{Q}_{3i}\leq\frac{18}{n}\left(R_{n,1}^{3}+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{n}}R_{n,2}^{3}+\frac{1}{6n^{2}}R_{n,3}^{3}\right)

(130)

Proof.

By
Lemma

18
and the fact that for any
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
:
|a+b|^{3}\leq 3\left(|a|^{3}+|b|^{3}\right)
, we have that:

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})\right\|_{L_{3}}^{3}\leq~

\displaystyle 3\left\|\bar{{\cal J}}_{i}^{\top}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}{{\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}}}\right\|^{3}_{L_{3}}+\frac{R_{n,3}^{3}}{72\,n^{3}}

(131)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle 9\left\|\bar{{\cal J}}_{i}^{\top}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\right\|_{L_{3}}^{3}+\frac{9}{8}\left\|\left(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}{{\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}}}\right\|^{3}_{L_{3}}+\frac{R_{n,3}^{3}}{72\,n^{3}}

(132)

Moreover, by
Lemma

9
we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\bar{{\cal J}}_{i}^{\top}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\right|^{3}\right]\leq~

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{k\leq d_{n}}\|\tilde{Y}_{i,k}^{c}\|_{L_{6}}\|\bar{{\cal J}}_{i,k}\|_{L_{6}}\right)^{3}\leq\frac{\left(R_{n,1}\right)^{3}}{n}

(133)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\left(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}{{\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}}}\right|^{3}\right]\leq~

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leq d_{n}}\|\tilde{Y}_{i,k_{1}}^{c}\|_{L_{9}}\|\tilde{Y}_{i,k_{2}}^{c}\|_{L_{9}}\|\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{1},k_{2}}\|_{L_{9}}\right)^{3}\leq\frac{(R_{n,2})^{3}}{n^{3/2}}

(134)

Combining the above yields the bound on
\|\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})\|_{L_{3}}^{3}
. The same bound on
\|\Delta_{i}(f_{n},Z_{i}^{n})\|_{L_{3}}^{3}
can be obtained in an identical manner. The lemma then follows.
∎

Lemma 20

(Bounding
\mathbb{Q}_{1i}
under (

H
1

))
.

For any statistic sequence
(f_{n})
, with
f_{n}\in\mathbb{C}^{3}
, which satisfies (

H
1

), we have for all
i\in[n]
:

\mathbb{Q}_{1i}\leq\frac{R_{n,3}}{3\,n}+\frac{2\,R_{n,2}}{n}

(135)

Proof.

Applying
Lemma

18
for
V=X_{\ell}^{n}
and
V=\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n}
and replacing the terms
\Delta_{i}(f_{n},X_{\ell}^{n})
and
\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})
in
\mathbb{Q}_{1i}
, with their corresponding second degree approximations, we have:

\mathbb{Q}_{1i}\leq\frac{R_{n,3}}{3n}+\left\|\bar{{\cal J}}_{i}^{\top}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n}\mid X^{n}\right]-\bar{X}^{n}\right)\right\|_{L_{1}}\\
+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left(X_{\ell}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}X_{\ell}^{c}-\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(136)

Importantly, observe that by the definition of
\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n}
, we have that
\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n}\mid X^{n}\right]=\bar{X}^{n}
. Thus the first order term in this expansion vanishes
. Hence:

\mathbb{Q}_{1i}\leq\frac{R_{n,3}}{3n}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left(X_{\ell}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}X_{\ell}^{c}-\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(137)

We can further split the second term on the right hand side as:

\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}_{1i}\leq\frac{R_{n,3}}{3n}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left(X_{\ell}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}X_{\ell}^{c}-\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Y_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}Y_{i}^{c}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}\\
+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Y_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}Y_{i}^{c}-\left(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(138)

Moreover by exploiting the independence of the observations
(X_{i}^{n})
we remark that

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{1i}^{(a)}:=~

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left(X_{\ell}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}X_{\ell}^{c}-\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Y_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}Y_{i}^{c}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(139)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leq d_{n}}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}X_{\ell,k_{1}}^{c}X_{\ell,k_{2}}^{c}-\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{i,k_{1}}^{c}Y_{i,k_{2}}^{c}\mid X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{2}}\,\left\|\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{1},k_{2}}\right\|_{L_{2}}

(140)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leq d_{n}}\sqrt{\text{Var}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell\leq n}X_{\ell,k_{1}}X_{\ell,k_{2}}\right]}\,\left\|\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{1},k_{2}}\right\|_{L_{2}}

(141)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leq d_{n}}\left\|\bar{X}^{n}_{k_{2}}\left(\bar{X}^{n}_{k_{1}}-\mathbb{E}\left[\bar{X}^{n}_{k_{1}}\right]\right)+\bar{X}^{n}_{k_{1}}\left(\bar{X}^{n}_{k_{2}}-\mathbb{E}\left[\bar{X}^{n}_{k_{2}}\right]\right)\right\|_{L_{2}}\,\left\|\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{1},k_{2}}\right\|_{L_{2}}

(142)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{3}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leq d_{n}}M_{k_{1}}^{n}M_{k_{2}}^{n}\left\|\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{1},k_{2}}\right\|_{L_{2}}

(143)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{3}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leq d_{n}}D_{k_{1:2}}(f_{n})\leq\frac{3}{n}R_{n,2}

(144)

Moreover, since for any two vectors
a,b
and symmetric matrix
M
, we have that:
a^{\top}Ma-b^{\top}Mb=(a-b)^{\top}Ma+b^{\top}M(b-a)
and since
Y_{i}^{c}-\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}=\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{n}]-\bar{X}^{n}
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{1i}^{(b)}:=~

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Y_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}Y_{i}^{c}-\left(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(145)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\bar{X}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}^{n}\right]\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}\,Y^{c}_{i}+\left(\tilde{Y}^{c}_{i}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}\left(\bar{X}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}^{n}\right]\right)\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(146)

Moreover, since
\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{i}^{c}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]=\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{n}]-\bar{X}^{n}
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]=0
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{1i}^{(b)}=~

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\bar{X}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}^{n}\right]\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}\,\left(\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}^{n}\right]-\bar{X}^{n}\right)\right\|_{L_{1}}

(147)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leq d_{n}}\left\|\bar{X}_{k_{1}}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1,k_{1}}^{n}\right]\right\|_{4}\,\left\|\bar{X}_{k_{2}}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1,k_{2}}^{n}\right]\right\|_{4}\,\left\|\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{1},k_{2}}\right\|_{L_{2}}

(148)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leq d_{n}}M_{k_{1}}^{n}M_{k_{2}}^{n}\left\|\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{1},k_{2}}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq\frac{1}{n^{3/2}}R_{n,2}

(149)

Combining
,
eq.

144
and
eq.

149
we obtain the result.
∎

Lemma 21

(Bounding
\mathbb{Q}_{2i}
under (

H
1

))
.

For any statistic sequence
(f_{n})
that satisfies
1
, we have for all
i\in[n]
:

\mathbb{Q}_{2i}\leq\frac{R_{n,3}}{3n^{4/3}}\left(4R_{n,1}+\frac{2}{n^{1/6}}R_{n,2}+\frac{1}{n^{2/3}}R_{n,3}\right)+\frac{3}{n^{7/6}}\left(\left(R_{n,1}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{n^{1/3}}\left(R_{n,2}\right)^{2}\right)

(150)

Proof.

For shorthand notation, let:

\displaystyle U(x)=~

\displaystyle\Delta_{i}(f_{n},x)

\displaystyle V(x)=~

\displaystyle\bar{{\cal J}}_{i}^{\top}x+\frac{1}{2}x^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}^{\top}x

(151)

We will use the fact that for any two random variables
U,V
:

\|U^{2}-V^{2}\|_{L_{1}}=\|(U-V)\,(U+V)\|_{L_{1}}\leq\|U-V\|_{L_{2}}\left(\|U\|_{L_{2}}+\|V\|_{L_{2}}\right).

(152)

We instantiate the latter with
U=U(X_{\ell}^{n})
and
V=V(X_{\ell}^{c})
. Then by
Lemma

18
, we then have that:

\|U-V\|_{L_{2}}\leq\frac{R_{n,3}}{6n}
. By
Lemma

19
, we have that
\|U\|_{L_{2}}\leq\frac{3}{n^{1/3}}\left(R_{n,1}+\frac{1}{2n^{1/6}}R_{n,2}+\frac{1}{2{n}^{2/3}}R_{n,3}\right)
. Moreover, by a sequence of triangle and Cauchy-Schwarz inequalities, we also have that:
\|V\|_{L_{2}}\leq\frac{R_{n,1}}{n^{1/3}}+\frac{R_{n,2}}{\sqrt{n}}
.
We can thus measure the approximation error of a second degree Taylor approximation:

\displaystyle\|U(X_{\ell}^{n})^{2}-V(X_{\ell}^{c})^{2}\|_{L_{1}}\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{R_{n,3}}{6n}\left(\frac{3}{n^{1/3}}\left(R_{n,1}+\frac{1}{2n^{1/6}}R_{n,2}+\frac{1}{2{n}^{2/3}}R_{n,3}\right)+\frac{R_{n,1}}{n^{1/3}}+\frac{R_{n,2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

(153)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{R_{n,3}}{6n^{4/3}}\left(4R_{n,1}+\frac{2}{n^{1/6}}R_{n,2}+\frac{1}{n^{2/3}}R_{n,3}\right)=:\epsilon_{n}

(154)

With identical steps the same bound holds for the analogous quantities
U(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n}),V(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c})
.
Therefore we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{2i}\leq~

\displaystyle 2\,\epsilon_{n}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell\leq n}V(X_{\ell}^{c})^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left[V(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c})^{2}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(155)

Moreover, if we denote
\tilde{X}_{\ell}^{n}:=X_{\ell}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}^{n}\right]
and
\zeta:=\bar{X}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}^{n}\right]
, then we have:

\left\|V(\tilde{X}^{n}_{\ell})^{2}-V(X^{c}_{\ell})^{2}\right\|_{L_{1}}\leq\left\|\big[\bar{{\cal J}}_{i}^{T}\zeta+\frac{1}{2}({X_{\ell}^{c}})^{T}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}\,\zeta+\frac{1}{2}\zeta^{T}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}\tilde{X}^{n}_{\ell}\right\|_{L_{2}}\,\left(\|V(X_{\ell}^{c})\|_{L_{2}}+[\|V(\tilde{X}_{\ell}^{n})\|_{L_{2}}\right)

(156)

Observe that the first term in the product on the right-hand side is at most
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\frac{R_{1,n}}{n^{1/3}}+\frac{R_{2,n}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)
; by applying a series of Cauchy–Schwarz and triangle inequalities, and invoking concentration of the vector
\zeta
, i.e.
\|\zeta_{k}\|_{L_{2}}\leq\frac{M_{k}^{n}}{\sqrt{n}}
. Moreover, each of the summands in the second term is at most
\left(\frac{R_{1,n}}{n^{1/3}}+\frac{R_{2,n}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)
; by Cauchy–Schwarz and traingle inequality. Thus we get:

\displaystyle\Big\|V(\tilde{X}^{n}_{\ell})^{2}-V(X^{c}_{\ell})^{2}\Big\|_{L_{1}}\leq\frac{2}{n^{7/6}}\left(R_{1,n}+\frac{1}{n^{1/6}}R_{2,n}\right)^{2}

(157)

Thus it suffices to upper bound the term:

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell\leq n}V(\tilde{X}_{\ell}^{n})^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left[V(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c})^{2}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(158)

Moreover, note that
\tilde{X}_{\ell}^{n}\overset{d}{=}\tilde{Y}_{\ell}^{c}
. Noting that by the form of
V
, we can expand the latter as:

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1:2}\leq d_{n}}\left\|\bar{{\cal J}}_{i,k_{1}}\bar{{\cal J}}_{i,k_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell\leq n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{1}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{2}}-\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{1}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{2}}\right]\right)\right\|_{L_{1}}

(159)

\displaystyle+2\sum_{k_{1:3}\leq d_{n}}\left\|\bar{{\cal J}}_{i,k_{1}}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{2},k_{3}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell\leq n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{1}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{2}}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{3}}-\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{1}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{2}}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{3}}\right]\right)\right\|_{L_{1}}

(160)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k_{1:4}\leq d_{n}}\left\|\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{1},k_{2}}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{3},k_{4}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell\leq n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{1}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{2}}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{3}}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{4}}-\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{1}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{2}}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{3}}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{4}}\right]\right)\right\|_{L_{1}}

(161)

By invoking Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and the concentration of each of the centered empirical averages, we have that the latter is bounded by:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k_{1:2}\leq d_{n}}\left\|\bar{{\cal J}}_{i,k_{1}}\right\|_{L_{4}}\left\|\bar{{\cal J}}_{i,k_{2}}\right\|_{L_{4}}M_{k_{1}}^{n}M_{k_{2}}^{n}+\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k_{1:3}\leq d_{n}}\left\|\bar{{\cal J}}_{i,k_{1}}\right\|_{L_{4}}\left\|\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{2},k_{3}}\right\|_{L_{4}}M_{k_{1}}^{n}M_{k_{2}}^{n}M_{k_{3}}^{n}

(162)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{k_{1:4}\leq d_{n}}\left\|\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{1},k_{2}}\right\|_{L_{4}}\left\|\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{3},k_{4}}\right\|_{L_{4}}M_{k_{1}}^{n}M_{k_{2}}^{n}M_{k_{3}}^{n}M_{k_{4}}^{n}

(163)

which in turn is upper bounded by:

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\sum_{k_{1}\leq d_{n}}\left\|\bar{{\cal J}}_{i,k_{1}}\right\|_{L_{4}}M_{k_{1}}^{n}+\sum_{k_{1:2}\leq d_{n}}\left\|\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{1},k_{2}}\right\|_{L_{4}}M_{k_{1}}^{n}M_{k_{2}}^{n}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\frac{R_{n,1}}{n^{1/3}}+\frac{R_{n,2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)^{2}

(164)

We can then conclude that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{2i}\leq~

\displaystyle 2\epsilon_{n}+\frac{3}{n^{7/6}}\left(\left(R_{n,1}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{n^{1/3}}\left(R_{n,2}\right)^{2}\right)

(165)

∎

Therefore by combining
Lemma

15
,
Lemma

16
,
Lemma

17
, with the three lemmas in this section we obtain that for some sufficiently large universal constant
K
:

\begin{split}\left\|d_{{\cal F}}\left(g_{n}(Z^{n}),g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}\right)\right\|_{L_{1}}\leq~&\left\|g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})-f_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\right\|_{L_{1}}+\left\|g_{n}(Z^{n})-f_{n}(Z^{n})\right\|_{L_{1}}\\
&+K\left(R_{n,3}+R_{n,2}+(R_{n,1})^{2}\max\left\{\frac{1}{n^{1/6}},R_{n,1}\right\}\right).\end{split}

C.3
Proof of
Theorem

13

Lemma 22

(Bounding
\mathbb{Q}_{3i}
under (

H
1

∗

))
.

For any statistic sequence
(f_{n})
that satisfies (

H
1

∗

), we have for all
i\in[n]
:

\displaystyle\max\left\{\|\Delta_{i}(f_{n},Z_{i}^{n})\|_{L_{3}}^{3},\,\|\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})\|_{L_{3}}^{3}\right\}\leq\frac{9}{n}\left(R_{n,1^{*}}^{3}+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{n}}R_{n,2^{*}}^{3}+\frac{1}{6{n}^{2}}R_{n,3^{*}}^{3}\right)

(166)

Therefore:

\mathbb{Q}_{3i}\leq\frac{18}{n}\left((R^{*}_{n,1})^{3}+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{n}}(R^{*}_{n,2})^{3}+\frac{1}{6{n}^{2}}(R^{*}_{n,3})^{3}\right)

(167)

Proof.

By
Lemma

18
and the fact that for any
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
:
|a+b|^{3}\leq 3\left(|a|^{3}+|b|^{3}\right)
, we have that:

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})\right\|_{L_{3}}^{3}\leq~

\displaystyle 3\left\|\bar{{\cal J}}_{i}^{\top}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}{{\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}}}\right\|^{3}_{L_{3}}+\frac{(R_{n,3}^{*})^{3}}{72\,n^{3}}

(168)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle 9\left\|\bar{{\cal J}}_{i}^{\top}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\right\|_{L_{3}}^{3}+\frac{9}{8}\left\|\left(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}{{\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}}}\right\|^{3}_{L_{3}}+\frac{(R_{n,3}^{*})^{3}}{72\,n^{3}}

(169)

Moreover, by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\bar{{\cal J}}_{i}^{\top}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\right|^{3}\right]\leq~

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{k\leq d_{n}}|\tilde{Y}^{c}_{i,k}|^{3}\left(\sum_{k\leq d_{n}}\big|\bar{{\cal J}}_{i,k}\big|\right)^{3}\right\|_{L_{1}}\leq\left\|\sup_{k\leq d_{n}}|\tilde{Y}^{c}_{i,k}|\right\|_{L_{6}}^{3}\left\|\sum_{k\leq d_{n}}\big|\bar{{\cal J}}_{i,k}\big|\right\|^{3}_{L_{6}}\leq\frac{\left(R^{*}_{n,1}\right)^{3}}{n}

(170)

By similar applications of the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality we obtain that:

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\left(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}{{\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}}}\right|^{3}\right]\leq~&\left(\left\|\sup_{k\leq d_{n}}\left|\tilde{Y}_{i,k}^{c}\right|\right\|^{2}_{L_{9}}\,\left\|\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leq d_{n}}\left|\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{1},k_{2}}\right|\right\|_{L_{9}}\right)^{3}\leq\frac{(R^{*}_{n,2})^{3}}{n^{3/2}}\end{split}

Combining the above yields the bound on
\|\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})\|_{L_{3}}^{3}
. The same bound on
\|\Delta_{i}(f_{n},Z_{i}^{n})\|_{L_{3}}^{3}
can be obtained in an identical manner. The lemma then follows.

∎

Lemma 23

(Bounding
\mathbb{Q}_{1i}
under (

H
1

∗

))
.

For any statistic sequence
(f_{n})
that satisfies (

H
1

∗

), we have that there is a constant
C
that does not depend on
n
such that for all
i\in[n]
:

\mathbb{Q}_{1i}\leq\frac{R^{*}_{n,3}}{3n}+\frac{C\max(\log(d_{n}),1)R_{2,n}^{*}}{n}

(171)

Proof.

Applying
Lemma

18
for
V=X_{\ell}^{n}
and
V=\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n}
and replacing the terms
\Delta_{i}(f_{n},X_{\ell}^{n})
and
\Delta_{i}(f_{n},\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})
in
\mathbb{Q}_{1i}
, with their corresponding second degree approximations, we have:

\mathbb{Q}_{1i}\leq\frac{R^{*}_{n,3}}{3n}+\left\|\bar{{\cal J}}_{i}^{\top}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n}\mid X^{n}\right]-\bar{X}^{n}\right)\right\|_{L_{1}}\\
+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left(X_{\ell}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}X_{\ell}^{c}-\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(172)

Importantly, observe that by the definition of
\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n}
, we have that
\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n}\mid X^{n}\right]=\bar{X}^{n}
. Thus the first order term in this expansion vanishes
. Hence:

\mathbb{Q}_{1i}\leq\frac{R^{*}_{n,3}}{3n}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left(X_{\ell}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}X_{\ell}^{c}-\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(173)

We can further split the second term on the right hand side as:

\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}_{1i}\leq\frac{R^{*}_{n,3}}{3n}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left(X_{\ell}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}X_{\ell}^{c}-\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Y_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}Y_{i}^{c}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}\\
+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Y_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}Y_{i}^{c}-\left(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(174)

Moreover by the triangular inequality we remark that

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{1i}^{(a)}:=~

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left(X_{\ell}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}X_{\ell}^{c}-\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Y_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}Y_{i}^{c}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(175)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{k_{1},k_{2}\leq d_{n}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}X_{\ell,k_{1}}^{c}X_{\ell,k_{2}}^{c}-\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{i,k_{1}}^{c}Y_{i,k_{2}}^{c}\mid X^{n}\right]\big|\right\|_{L_{2}}\,\left\|\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leq d_{n}}\big|\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{1},k_{2}}\big|\right\|_{L_{2}}

(176)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{k_{1},k_{2}\leq d_{n}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}X^{n}_{\ell,k_{1}}X^{n}_{\ell,k_{2}}-\mathbb{E}\left[Y^{n}_{i,k_{1}}Y^{n}_{i,k_{2}}\mid X^{n}\right]\big|\right\|_{L_{2}}\,\left\|\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leq d_{n}}\big|\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{1},k_{2}}\big|\right\|_{L_{2}}

(177)

\displaystyle~+

\displaystyle~2\left\|\sup_{k_{1},k_{2}\leq d_{n}}\big|\bar{X}^{n}_{k_{1}}\big|\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}X^{n}_{\ell,k_{2}}-\mathbb{E}\left[Y^{n}_{i,k_{2}}\mid X^{n}\right]\big|\right\|_{L_{2}}\,\left\|\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leq d_{n}}\big|\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{1},k_{2}}\big|\right\|_{L_{2}}

(178)

Using
lemma

8
we know that there is a constant
C\in\mathbb{R}
that does not depend on
n
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{k_{1},k_{2}\leq d_{n}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}X^{n}_{\ell,k_{1}}X^{n}_{\ell,k_{2}}-\mathbb{E}\left[Y^{n}_{i,k_{1}}Y^{n}_{i,k_{2}}\mid X^{n}\right]\big|\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq\frac{C\log(d_{n})}{\sqrt{n}}\big\|\sup_{k_{1}}|X^{n}_{l,k_{1}}|\big\|_{L_{4}}^{2};

(179)

and such that

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{k_{1},k_{2}\leq d_{n}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}X^{n}_{\ell,k_{2}}-\mathbb{E}\left[Y^{n}_{i,k_{2}}\mid X^{n}\right]\big|\right\|_{L_{4}}\leq\frac{C\log(d_{n})}{\sqrt{n}}\big\|\sup_{k_{1}}|X^{n}_{l,k_{1}}|\big\|_{L_{4}}.

(180)

Therefore we can upper-bound
eq.

178
as:

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left(X_{\ell}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}X_{\ell}^{c}-\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Y_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}Y_{i}^{c}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}\leq\frac{3\max\big[1,C\log(d_{n})\big]R_{2,n}^{*}}{n}

(181)

Moreover, since for any two vectors
a,b
and symmetric matrix
M
, we have that:
a^{\top}Ma-b^{\top}Mb=(a-b)^{\top}Ma+b^{\top}M(b-a)
and since
Y_{i}^{c}-\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}=\mathbb{E}[X_{1}^{n}]-\bar{X}^{n}
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{1i}^{(b)}:=~

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Y_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}Y_{i}^{c}-\left(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(182)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\bar{X}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}^{n}\right]\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}\,Y^{c}_{i}+\left(\tilde{Y}^{c}_{i}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}\left(\bar{X}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}^{n}\right]\right)\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(183)

As we established that
\left\|\sup_{k\leq d_{n}}\big|\bar{X}_{k}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1,k}^{n}\right]\big|\right\|_{L_{4}}\leq C\log(d_{n})\left\|\sup_{k\leq d_{n}}\big|X_{i,k}\big|\right\|_{L_{4}}
we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{1i}^{(b)}\leq~

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\bar{X}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}^{n}\right]\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}\,Y^{c}_{i}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}+\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\tilde{Y}^{c}_{i}\right)^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}\left(\bar{X}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}^{n}\right]\right)\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(184)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{k\leq d_{n}}\big|\bar{X}_{k}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1,k}^{n}\right]\big|\right\|_{L_{3}}\,\left\|\sup_{k\leq d_{n}}\big|Y_{i,k}^{c}\big|+\big|\tilde{Y}_{i,k}^{c}\big|\right\|_{L_{3}}\,\left\|\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leq d_{n}}\big|\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{1},k_{2}}\big|\right\|_{L_{3}}

(185)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{4C\log(d_{n})}{n}\left\|\sup_{k\leq d_{n}}\big|X_{i,k}\big|\right\|^{2}_{L_{4}}\left\|\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\leq d_{n}}\big|\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{1},k_{2}}\big|\right\|_{L_{3}}

(186)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{2C\log(d_{n})R_{2,n}^{*}}{n}

(187)

Combining
,
eq.

181
and
eq.

187
we obtain the result.
∎

Lemma 24

(Bounding
\mathbb{Q}_{2i}
under (

H
1

∗

))
.

For any statistic sequence
(f_{n})
that satisfies
Equation

H
1

∗

, we have that there is a constant
C
that does not depend on
n
such that for all
i\in[n]
:

\mathbb{Q}_{2i}\leq\frac{R^{*}_{n,3}}{3n^{4/3}}\left(4R^{*}_{n,1}+\frac{2}{n^{1/6}}R^{*}_{n,2}+\frac{1}{n^{2/3}}R^{*}_{n,3}\right)+\frac{C\max(\log(d_{n}),1)}{n^{7/6}}\left(\left(R^{*}_{n,1}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{n^{1/3}}\left(R^{*}_{n,2}\right)^{2}\right)

(188)

Proof.

For shorthand notation, let:

\displaystyle U(x)=~

\displaystyle\Delta_{i}(f_{n},x)

\displaystyle V(x)=~

\displaystyle\bar{{\cal J}}_{i}^{\top}x+\frac{1}{2}x^{\top}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}^{\top}x

(189)

We will use the fact that for any two random variables
U,V
:

\|U^{2}-V^{2}\|_{L_{1}}=\|(U-V)\,(U+V)\|_{L_{1}}\leq\|U-V\|_{L_{2}}\left(\|U\|_{L_{2}}+\|V\|_{L_{2}}\right).

(190)

We instantiate the latter with
U=U(X_{\ell}^{n})
and
V=V(X_{\ell}^{c})
. Then by
Lemma

18
, we then have that:

\|U-V\|_{L_{2}}\leq\frac{R^{*}_{n,3}}{6n}
. By
Lemma

22
, we have that
\|U\|_{L_{2}}\leq\frac{3}{n^{1/3}}\left(R^{*}_{n,1}+\frac{1}{2n^{1/6}}R^{*}_{n,2}+\frac{1}{2{n}^{2/3}}R^{*}_{n,3}\right)
. Moreover, by a sequence of triangle and Cauchy-Schwarz inequalities, we also have that:
\|V\|_{L_{2}}\leq\frac{R^{*}_{n,1}}{n^{1/3}}+\frac{R^{*}_{n,2}}{\sqrt{n}}
.
We can thus measure the approximation error of a second degree Taylor approximation:

\displaystyle\|U(X_{\ell}^{n})^{2}-V(X_{\ell}^{c})^{2}\|_{L_{1}}\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{R^{*}_{n,3}}{6n}\left(\frac{3}{n^{1/3}}\left(R^{*}_{n,1}+\frac{1}{2n^{1/6}}R^{*}_{n,2}+\frac{1}{2{n}^{2/3}}R^{*}_{n,3}\right)+\frac{R^{*}_{n,1}}{n^{1/3}}+\frac{R^{*}_{n,2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

(191)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{R^{*}_{n,3}}{6n^{4/3}}\left(4R^{*}_{n,1}+\frac{2}{n^{1/6}}R^{*}_{n,2}+\frac{1}{n^{2/3}}R^{*}_{n,3}\right)=:\epsilon_{n}

(192)

With identical steps the same bound holds for the analogous quantities
U(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n}),V(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c})
.
Therefore we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{2i}\leq~

\displaystyle 2\,\epsilon_{n}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell\leq n}V(X_{\ell}^{c})^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left[V(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c})^{2}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(193)

Moreover, if we denote
\tilde{X}_{\ell}^{n}:=X_{\ell}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}^{n}\right]
and
\zeta:=\bar{X}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}^{n}\right]
, then we have:

\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\Big\|V(\tilde{X}^{n}_{\ell})^{2}-V(X^{c}_{\ell})^{2}\Big\|_{L_{1}}\\
\leq\left\|\big[\bar{{\cal J}}_{i}^{T}\zeta+\frac{1}{2}({X_{\ell}^{c}})^{T}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}\,\zeta+\frac{1}{2}\zeta^{T}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i}\tilde{X}^{n}_{\ell}\right\|_{L_{2}}\,\left(\|V(X_{\ell}^{c})\|_{L_{2}}+[\|V(\tilde{X}_{\ell}^{n})\|_{L_{2}}\right)

(194)

Observe that by
Lemma

8
we know that there is a constant
C
that does not depend on
n
such that:
\|\sup_{k}\zeta_{k}\|_{L_{6}}\leq C\log(d_{n})\frac{\|\sup_{k}|X^{n}_{1,k}|\|_{L_{6}}}{\sqrt{n}}
. Therefore, by applying a series of Cauchy–Schwarz and triangle inequalities, the first term in the product on the right-hand side of
is at most
\frac{C\log(d_{n})}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\frac{R^{*}_{1,n}}{n^{1/3}}+\frac{R^{*}_{2,n}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)
.Thus we get:

\displaystyle\Big\|V(\tilde{X}^{n}_{\ell})^{2}-V(X^{c}_{\ell})^{2}\Big\|_{L_{1}}\leq\frac{2C\log(d_{n})}{n^{7/6}}\left(R^{*}_{1,n}+\frac{1}{n^{1/6}}R^{*}_{2,n}\right)^{2}

(195)

Thus it suffices to upper bound the term:

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell\leq n}V(\tilde{X}_{\ell}^{n})^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left[V(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{c})^{2}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}

(196)

Moreover, note that
\tilde{X}_{\ell}^{n}\overset{d}{=}\tilde{Y}_{\ell}^{c}
. Noting that by the form of
V
, we can expand the latter as:

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{k_{1:2}\leq d_{n}}\Big|\bar{{\cal J}}_{i,k_{1}}\bar{{\cal J}}_{i,k_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell\leq n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{1}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{2}}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{1}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{2}}^{n}\right]\right)\Big|\right\|_{L_{1}}

(197)

\displaystyle+2\left\|\sum_{k_{1:3}\leq d_{n}}\Big|\bar{{\cal J}}_{i,k_{1}}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{2},k_{3}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell\leq n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{1}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{2}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{3}}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{1}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{2}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{3}}^{n}\right]\right)\Big|\right\|_{L_{1}}

(198)

\displaystyle+\left\|\sum_{k_{1:4}\leq d_{n}}\Big|\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{1},k_{2}}\bar{{\cal H}}_{i,k_{2},k_{3}}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell\leq n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{1}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{2}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{3}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{4}}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{1}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{2}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{3}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{4}}^{n}\right]\right)\Big|\right\|_{L_{1}}

(199)

By invoking
lemma

8
and Cauchy–Schwarz we can find
C^{\prime}
such that:

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{k_{1:2}\leq d_{n}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell\leq n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{1}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{2}}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{1}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{2}}^{n}\right]\right|\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}\log(d_{n})\left\|\sup_{k\leq d_{n}}\big|X_{l,k}^{n}\big|\right\|_{L_{4}}^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}

(200)

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{k_{1:3}\leq d_{n}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell\leq n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{1}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{2}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{3}}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{1}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{2}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{3}}^{n}\right]\right|\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}\log(d_{n})\left\|\sup_{k\leq d_{n}}\big|X_{l,k}^{n}\big|\right\|_{L_{6}}^{3}}{\sqrt{n}}

(201)

\displaystyle+\left\|\sup_{k_{1:4}\leq d_{n}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell\leq n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{1}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{2}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{3}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{4}}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{1}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{2}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{3}}^{n}\tilde{X}_{\ell,k_{4}}^{n}\right]\right|\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}\log(d_{n})\left\|\sup_{k\leq d_{n}}\big|X_{l,k}^{n}\big|\right\|_{L_{8}}^{4}}{\sqrt{n}}.

(202)

Therefore by Cauchy–Schwarz inequality we have that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell\leq n}V(\tilde{X}_{\ell}^{n})^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left[V(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n})^{2}\mid Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}},X^{n}\right]\right\|_{L_{1}}\leq\frac{\max(C^{\prime}\log(d_{n}),1)}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\frac{R^{*}_{n,1}}{n^{1/3}}+\frac{R^{*}_{n,2}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)^{2}.

(203)

We can then conclude that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{2i}\leq~

\displaystyle 2\epsilon_{n}+\frac{2\max((C^{\prime}+C)\log(d_{n}),1)}{n^{7/6}}\left(\left(R^{*}_{n,1}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{n^{1/3}}\left(R^{*}_{n,2}\right)^{2}\right)

(204)

∎

Therefore by combining
Lemma

15
,
Lemma

16
,
Lemma

17
, with the three lemmas in this section we obtain that for some sufficiently large universal constant
K
:

\begin{split}\left\|d_{{\cal F}}\left(g_{n}(Z^{n}),g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}\right)\right\|_{L_{1}}\leq~&\left\|g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})-f_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\right\|_{L_{1}}+\left\|g_{n}(Z^{n})-f_{n}(Z^{n})\right\|_{L_{1}}\\
&+K\left(\log(d_{n})\left((R_{n,1}^{*})^{2}\max\left\{\frac{1}{n^{1/6}},R^{*}_{n,1}\right\}+R_{n,2}^{*}\right)+R_{n,3}^{*}\right).\end{split}

C.4
Proof of
1

Proof.

Firstly, we remark that by using
Lemma

10
we have:

\displaystyle\left\|d_{\mathcal{F}}\big(g_{n}(Z^{n})-\mathbb{E}(g_{n}(Z^{n})\mid X^{n}),~g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})-\mathbb{E}(g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n})\mid X^{n}\big)\right\|_{L_{1}}

(205)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|d_{\mathcal{F}}\big(g_{n}(Z^{n})-\mathbb{E}(g_{n}(Z^{n})\mid X^{n}),~g_{n}(Z^{n})-\mathbb{E}(g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n})\mid X^{n}\big)\right\|_{L_{1}}

(206)

\displaystyle+\left\|d_{\mathcal{F}}\big(g_{n}(Z^{n})-\mathbb{E}(g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}),~g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})-\mathbb{E}(g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n})\mid X^{n}\big)\right\|_{L_{1}}

(207)

\displaystyle\leq(A)+(B)

(208)

In the goal of bounding
(A)
, denote
h:x\rightarrow x
the identity function. We easily note that
h
belongs to the function class
\mathcal{F}
. Indeed it is three times differentiable with all its derivatives bounded by
1
. Therefore by
theorem

1
we have

(A)\leq\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\mathbb{E}\left(g_{n}(Z^{n})-g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}\right)\right|\right)\leq\left\|d_{\mathcal{F}}\big(g_{n}(Z^{n}),g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}\big)\right\|_{L_{1}}.

To upper-bound
(B)
we note that
Lemma

11
guarantees that:

\displaystyle\left\|d_{\mathcal{F}}\big(g_{n}(Z^{n})-\mathbb{E}(g_{n}(Z^{n})\mid X^{n}),~g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})-\mathbb{E}(g_{n}(Z^{n})\mid X^{n})\mid X^{n}\big)\right\|_{L_{1}}

(209)

\displaystyle=\left\|d_{\mathcal{F}}\big(g_{n}(Z^{n}),~g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}\big)\right\|_{L_{1}}

(210)

This implies that
(A)+(B)\leq 2\left\|d_{\mathcal{F}}\big(g_{n}(Z^{n}),~g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}\big)\right\|_{L_{1}}
which proves the desired result.

∎

Appendix D
Proofs from
Section

2

D.1
Proof of
1

Proof.

We choose a measurable subset
A\subset\mathbb{R}
and define the characteristic function

f:x\rightarrow\mathbb{I}(x\in A_{3\epsilon}).

(211)

Choose
\epsilon>0
and set
h_{\epsilon}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
to be the following three-times differentiable function:

h_{\epsilon}(x):=\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}\int_{x-\epsilon}^{x}\int_{t-\epsilon}^{t}\int_{y-\epsilon}^{y}f(z)dz\,dy\,dt.

By simple observation we obtain that
\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\max_{i\leq 3}\big|h^{(i)}_{\epsilon}(x)\big|\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}.

Therefore we have for any two random variables
U,V
and any event
{\cal E}
:

{\mathbb{E}\left[h_{\epsilon}(V)\mid{\cal E}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[h_{\epsilon}(U)\mid{\cal E}\right]}\leq\frac{d_{{\cal F}}(U,V\mid{\cal E})}{\epsilon^{3}}.

(212)

Moreover, we remark that
h_{\epsilon}(x)\neq 0
only if
x\in A_{6\epsilon}
and that
h_{\epsilon}(x)=1
if
x\in A
. Thus for any random variable
Z
:

\mathbb{E}[h_{\epsilon}(Z)\mid{\cal E}]\in\left[\Pr(Z\in A\mid{\cal E}),\Pr(Z\in A_{6\epsilon}\mid{\cal E})\right]

(213)

which then implies that:

{\mathbb{E}\left[h_{\epsilon}(V)\mid{\cal E}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[h_{\epsilon}(U)\mid{\cal E}\right]}\geq P(V\in A\mid{\cal E})-P(U\in A_{6\epsilon}\mid{\cal E}).

(214)

Thus we have that for any two random variables
U,V
:

P(U\in A_{6\epsilon}\mid{\cal E})\geq P(V\in A\mid{\cal E})-\frac{d_{{\cal F}}(U,V\mid{\cal E})}{\epsilon^{3}}

(215)

Finally we observe that since for any
h\in{\cal F}
, we have
|h^{\prime}(u)|\leq 1
for all
u
, we have that
h(X-\mathbb{E}[X\mid{\cal E}])\leq h(X)+\mathbb{E}[X\mid{\cal E}]
and
h(Y-\mathbb{E}[Y])\geq h(Y)-\mathbb{E}[Y\mid{\cal E}]
. Thus:

\displaystyle d_{{\cal F}}(X-\mathbb{E}\left[X\mid{\cal E}\right],Y-\mathbb{E}\left[Y\mid{\cal E}\right]\mid{\cal E})=~

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in{\cal F}}\mathbb{E}[h(X-\mathbb{E}[X\mid{\cal E}])\mid{\cal E}]-\mathbb{E}[h(Y-\mathbb{E}[Y\mid{\cal E}])\mid{\cal E}]

(216)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in{\cal F}}\mathbb{E}[h(X)\mid{\cal E}]-\mathbb{E}[h(Y)\mid{\cal E}]+\mathbb{E}[X\mid{\cal E}]-\mathbb{E}[Y\mid{\cal E}]\big|

(217)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in{\cal F}}\mathbb{E}[h(X)\mid{\cal E}]-\mathbb{E}[h(Y)\mid{\cal E}]+\sup_{h\in{\cal F}}\mathbb{E}[h(X)\mid{\cal E}]-\mathbb{E}[h(Y)\mid{\cal E}]

(218)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle 2\,d_{{\cal F}}(X,Y\mid{\cal E})

(219)

Thus applying Equation (
215
) to the centered random variables and invoking the fact that
\Pr(Y-\mathbb{E}[Y]\in A)\geq 1-\alpha
, we get that:

P(X-\mathbb{E}[X\mid{\cal E}]\in A_{6\epsilon}\mid{\cal E})\geq P(Y-\mathbb{E}[Y\mid{\cal E}]\in A\mid{\cal E})-\frac{2d_{{\cal F}}(X,Y\mid{\cal E})}{\epsilon^{3}}\geq 1-\alpha-\frac{2d_{{\cal F}}(X,Y\mid{\cal E})}{\epsilon^{3}}

(220)

which concludes the proof of the proposition.

∎

Appendix E
Further Proofs from
Section

3

E.1
Proof of
Example

3.2

Proof.

For simplicity, we write:

\displaystyle J_{i}:=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}(\min_{j\neq i}|X_{j}-X_{i}|>1/n)

\displaystyle g_{n}(X_{1:n}):=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\leq n}J_{i}-\mathbb{E}(J_{i})

(221)

Moreover we note that for all
i\neq j
we have
\tilde{Z}_{i}-\tilde{Z}_{j}=Z_{i}-Z_{j}
this implies that

g_{n}(\tilde{Z}_{1:n})=g_{n}(Z_{1:n}).

It is therefore enough to study
g_{n}(Z_{1:n})
. We remark that
g_{n}
is stable in the perturbation of one of the observations, since changing the value of
X_{1}
can change at most
3
of the random variables
(J_{i})
. To make this rigorous, we denote the distance to closest neighbour of
x\in[0,1]
, larger than
x
, as
d^{+}(x)={\min}_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\geq 2,X_{j}\geq x\end{subarray}}\mid X_{j}-x\mid
and to the closest neighbour, smaller than
x
, as
d^{-}(x)={\min}_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\geq 2,X_{j}\leq x\end{subarray}}\mid X_{j}-x\mid
. By convention, if there is no
j\geq 2
such that
X_{j}\geq x
(respectively
X_{j}\leq x
) then we take
d^{+}(x)
to be
0
(respectively
d^{-}(x)=0
). We then have

\displaystyle\big\|g_{n}(X_{1:n})-g_{n}(0X_{2:n})\Big\|_{L_{3}}\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\big\|J_{1}-\mathbb{I}(\min_{j\geq 2}|X_{j}|>1/n)\Big\|_{L_{3}}+\big\|d^{+}(X_{1})-d^{+}(0)\big\|_{L_{3}}+\big\|d^{-}(X_{1})\big\|_{L_{3}}\right)

(222)

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}~

\displaystyle\frac{3}{\sqrt{n}}

(223)

We show that
d_{{\cal F}}\Big(g_{n}(Y^{n}),\,g_{n}(Z^{n})|X^{n}\Big)
does not go to
0
. We prove it by contradiction. Suppose that
\|d_{{\cal F}}\Big(g_{n}(Y^{n}),g_{n}(Z^{n})|X^{n}\Big)\|_{L_{1}}\rightarrow 0
;,
as the random variables
(g_{n}(Y^{n}))
and
(g_{n}(Z^{n}))
are uniformly integrable we have that
\mathbb{E}\left[g_{n}(Y^{n})\mid X^{n}\right]
converges to
\mathbb{E}\left[g_{n}(Z^{n})\mid X^{n}\right]
. Moreover, we note that by the definition of
g_{n}
:
\mathbb{E}\left[g_{n}(Y^{n})\mid X^{n}\right]=0
.
We remark that

P\left(\min_{i\neq 1}|X_{i}-X_{1}|\geq 1/n\right)=\left(1-\frac{1}{n}\right)^{n-1}+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)=e^{-1}+o(1/\sqrt{n}).

(224)

Moreover if we denote
C^{n}_{X_{i}}:={\rm card}\left(j\neq i~{\rm s.t}~|X_{i}-X_{j}|\leq\frac{1}{n}\right)
we have

P\left(\min_{i\neq 1}|Z^{n}_{i}-Z^{n}_{1}|\geq 1/n\mid X^{n}\right)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\leq n}e^{-1-C^{n}_{X_{i}}}+o(1/\sqrt{n}).

(225)

This notably implies that:

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[g_{n}(Z^{n})\mid X^{n}\right]=~&\sqrt{n}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\leq n}e^{-1-C^{n}_{X_{i}}}-e^{-1}\right)+o(1)\\
=~&\sqrt{n}e^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\leq n}e^{-C^{n}_{X_{i}}}-1\right)+o(1)\end{split}

We show that
\mathbb{E}\left[g_{n}(Z^{n})\mid X^{n}\right]
is asymptotically non-positive and takes asymptotically, strictly negative values with non-zero probability. This would then imply that
\mathbb{E}\left[g_{n}(Z^{n})\mid X^{n}\right]
is not asymptotically converging to
\mathbb{E}\left[g_{n}(Y^{n})\right]=0
, which contradicts the fact that
\left\|d_{{\cal F}}\left(g_{n}(Y^{n}),g_{n}(Z^{n})\mid X^{n}\right)\right\|_{L_{1}}\rightarrow 0
. The first part follows since,
e^{-C_{X_{i}}^{n}}\leq 1
, and we therefore have that
\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[g_{n}(Z^{n})\mid X^{n}\right]\leq 0
. For the second part, we note that it is enough to lower bound the probability that
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\leq n}e^{-C^{n}_{X_{i}}}-1
is strictly negative. We observe that
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\leq n}e^{-C^{n}_{X_{i}}}-1
is bounded by
1
and that

\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-C^{n}_{X_{1}}}\right]
is the moment generating function of a binomial distribution with
n-1
trials and success probability at most
2/n
. This is at most
\left(1-2/n+2\,e^{-1}/n\right)^{n-1}\leq e^{-(2-2/e)\,(n-1)/n}\leq e^{-1.2}+o(1)
. Thus for sufficiently large
n
, we have that with probability bounded away from zero:
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\leq n}e^{-C^{n}_{X_{i}}}-1<0
, implying:

\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}P\left(\mathbb{E}\left[g_{n}(Z^{n})\mid X^{n}\right]<0\right)>0;

Thus
\left\|d_{{\cal F}}\left(g_{n}(Y^{n}),g_{n}(Z^{n})\mid X^{n}\right)\right\|_{L_{1}}\nrightarrow 0
and the bootstrap method is not consistent.
∎

E.2
Proof of
Theorem

2

Proof.

Let
(Y^{n}_{i})
be an independent copy of
(X^{n}_{i})
. Since, by
Lemma

10
,
d_{{\cal F}}
satisfies the triangular inequality:

\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\left\|d_{{\cal F}}\left(g_{n}(Z^{n})-\mathbb{E}[g_{n}(Z^{n})|X^{n}],\,g_{n}(Y^{n})-\mathbb{E}[g_{n}(Y^{n})]\mid X^{n}\right)\right\|_{L_{1}}\\
\leq\left\|d_{{\cal F}}\left(g_{n}(Z^{n})-\mathbb{E}[g_{n}(Z^{n})|X^{n}],~g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})-\mathbb{E}[g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})|X^{n}],~\mid X^{n}\right)\right\|_{L_{1}}\\
+\left\|d_{{\cal F}}\left(g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})-\mathbb{E}[g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})|X^{n}],~g_{n}(Y^{n})-\mathbb{E}[g_{n}(Y^{n})]\mid X^{n}\right)\right\|_{L_{1}}=:\mathbb{I}_{1}+\mathbb{I}_{2}

(226)

The first term
\mathbb{I}_{1}
can be upper-bounded using
1
. We therefore focus on bounding the second term
\mathbb{I}_{2}
of Equation

Let
(B_{n})
be a an increasing sequence such that (i)
B_{n}\rightarrow\infty
and (ii)
r^{n,B_{n}}\rightarrow 0
. We remark that under
2
such a sequence always exists.
For example set
B_{1}=1
and
L_{1}=1
; then for all
n
if
r^{n,2\,B_{n}}\leq 2^{-L_{n}}
then set
L_{n+1}=L_{n}+1
and
B_{n+1}=2\,B_{n}
(by
2
the latter will occur at some finite
n
); otherwise keep
B_{n+1}=B_{n}
and
L_{n+1}=L_{n}
.

We note that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}_{2}\leq~

\displaystyle\left\|g_{n}(Y^{n})-\mathbb{E}[g_{n}(Y^{n})]-\Big(g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})-\mathbb{E}[g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}]\Big)\right\|_{L_{1}}

(227)

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}~

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathbb{I}(\sqrt{n}\big\|\bar{X}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}^{n}\right]\|_{2}\leq B_{n})

(228)

\displaystyle\times\sup_{x\in B_{d_{n}}(0,B_{n})}\left|g_{n}\left(Y^{n}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)-\mathbb{E}\Big[g_{n}\left(Y^{n}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\mid X^{n}\Big]-g_{n}(Y^{n})+\mathbb{E}\big[g_{n}\left(Y^{n}\right)\big]\right|\Big\|_{L_{1}}

(229)

\displaystyle+\Big\|\mathbb{I}(\sqrt{n}\big\|\bar{X}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}^{n}\right]\|_{2}\geq B_{n})\times\Big[g_{n}(Y^{n})-\mathbb{E}[g_{n}(Y^{n})]-\big(g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})-\mathbb{E}[g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}]\big)\Big]\Big\|_{L_{1}}

(230)

where (a) is a consequence of the triangle inequality. The first term is bounded by

\left\|\sup_{x\in B_{d_{n}}(0,B_{n})}\left|g_{n}\left(Y^{n}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)-\mathbb{E}\Big[g_{n}\left(Y^{n}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\mid X^{n}\Big]-g_{n}(Y^{n})+\mathbb{E}\big[g_{n}\left(Y^{n}\right)\big]\right|\right\|_{L_{1}}\leq r^{n,B_{n}}.

The second term can be bounded by the use of the Cauchy-Swartz inequality:

\Big\|\mathbb{I}(\sqrt{n}\big\|\bar{X}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}^{n}\right]\|_{2}\geq B_{n})\times\Big[g_{n}(Y^{n})-\mathbb{E}[g_{n}(Y^{n})]-\big(g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})-\mathbb{E}[g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}]\big)\Big]\Big\|_{L_{1}}\\
\leq\Big\|\mathbb{I}\left\{\sqrt{n}\big\|\bar{X}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}^{n}\right]\|_{2}\geq B_{n}\right\}\Big\|_{L_{2}}\left(\left\|g_{n}(Y^{n})\right\|_{L_{2}}+\left\|g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\right\|_{L_{2}}\right)

(231)

Observe that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\bar{X}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}^{n}\right]\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k\leq d_{n}}\left(\bar{X}_{k}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1,k}^{n}\right]\right)^{2}\right]

(232)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\leq d_{n}}\text{Var}\left(\bar{X}_{k}^{n}\right)\leq\frac{4\sum_{k\leq d_{n}}\|X_{1,k}^{n}\|_{L_{2}}^{2}}{n}

(233)

Hence, by Chebyshev’s inequality:

\displaystyle P\left(\left\|\bar{X}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}^{n}\right]\right\|_{2}\geq B_{n}/\sqrt{n}\right)\leq\frac{n\text{Var}\left(\left\|\bar{X}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}^{n}\right]\right\|_{2}\right)}{B_{n}^{2}}\leq\frac{4\sum_{k\leq d_{n}}\|X_{1,k}^{n}\|_{L_{2}}^{2}}{B_{n}^{2}}

(234)

Thus we have:

\left\|\mathbb{I}\left\{\sqrt{n}\left\|\bar{X}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}^{n}\right]\right\|_{2}\geq B_{n}\right\}\right\|_{L_{2}}\leq\sqrt{P\left(\left\|\bar{X}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}^{n}\right]\right\|_{2}\geq B_{n}/\sqrt{n}\right)}\leq\frac{2\sqrt{\sum_{k\leq k_{n}}\|X_{1,k}^{n}\|_{L_{2}}^{2}}}{B_{n}}

(235)

Thus we conclude that:

\left\|d_{{\cal F}}\left(g_{n}(Y^{n})-\mathbb{E}[g_{n}(Y^{n})],g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})-\mathbb{E}[g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}]\mid X^{n}\right)\right\|_{L_{1}}\\
\leq\frac{2\sqrt{\sum_{k\leq k_{n}}\|X_{1,k}^{n}\|_{L_{2}}^{2}}}{B_{n}}\left(\left\|g_{n}(Y^{n})\right\|_{L_{2}}+\left\|g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\right\|_{L_{2}}\right)+r^{n,B_{n}}.

(236)

∎

E.3
Proof of
Example

3.3

Proof.

We remark that both
g_{n,1}
and
g_{n,2}
are invariant under uniform perturbations. Indeed for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
we have:

\begin{split}g_{n,1}(X_{1:n}+x/\sqrt{n})&=\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\leq\lfloor n/2\rfloor}X_{i}-\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}-\left(x_{i+\lfloor n/2\rfloor}-\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\right)^{2}=g_{n,1}(X_{1:n});\end{split}

and

\begin{split}g_{n,2}(X_{1:n}+x/\sqrt{n})&=\sqrt{n}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(1+\frac{x_{i}-{x/\sqrt{n}}-\big(\bar{x}^{n}-x/\sqrt{n}\big)}{n}\right)-1\right)=g_{n,2}(X_{1:n}).\end{split}

Therefore both functions
(g_{n,1})
and
(g_{n,2})
satisfy (

H
2

). Moreover by a direct application of the chain rule we can verify that both
(g_{n,1})
and
(g_{n,2})
verify conditions
(H_{0})
and
(H_{1})
. Hence
Theorem

2
implies that:

d_{{\cal F}}\left(g_{n,1}(Z^{n})-\mathbb{E}(g_{n,1}(Z^{n})|X),g_{n,1}(Y_{1:n})-\mathbb{E}(g_{n,1}(Y_{1:n}))\right)\rightarrow 0;

d_{{\cal F}}\left(g_{n,2}(Z^{n})-\mathbb{E}(g_{n,2}(Z^{n})|X),g_{n,2}(Y_{1:n})-\mathbb{E}(g_{n,2}(Y_{1:n}))\right)\rightarrow 0.

∎

E.4
Proof of
Theorem

3

Proof.

The proof works by contradiction. Suppose that there is a measurable function
\mathcal{Q}_{n}:x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{M}(\mathbb{R})
such that for all sequence of measures
(\nu_{n})\in\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{P}_{n}
the following holds:

\Big\|d_{{\cal F}}\Big(\mathcal{Q}_{n}(X^{n}),g_{n}(Y^{n})-\mathbb{E}(g_{n}(Y^{n}))\mid X^{n}\Big)\Big\|_{L_{1}}\xrightarrow{n\rightarrow\infty}0.

By hypothesis we know that there is a sequence of measures
(p_{\theta_{n}}^{n})\in\times_{l=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{P}_{n}
, an
\epsilon>0
and and a sequence of vectors
(z_{n})
such that

(i.)

\limsup\sup_{\tilde{\theta}_{n}\in[\theta_{n},\theta_{n}+\frac{z_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}]}~~\|\mathcal{I}_{n}(\tilde{\theta}_{n})^{1/2}\frac{z_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\|_{v,d_{n}}<\infty
;

(ii.)

The following holds for
(X^{n})\overset{i.i.d}{\sim}{p_{\theta_{n}}^{n}}

d_{{\cal F}}\left(g_{n}\left(X^{n}+\frac{z_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{n}\left(X^{n}+\frac{z_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\right],g_{n}(X^{n})-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{n}(X^{n})\right]\right)>\epsilon.

(237)

(iii.)

\theta_{n}+\frac{z_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\in\Omega_{n}

By abuse of notations we denote
p_{\theta_{n}}^{n}+\frac{z_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}
the distribution of
X+\frac{z_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}
for
X\sim p_{\theta_{n}}^{n}
. We define the following subset of distributions
\mathcal{P}_{n}^{*}:=\{p_{\theta_{n}}^{n},p_{\theta_{n}}^{n}+\frac{z_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\}
and let
X^{n}
be i.i.d random variables distributed according to
\mu_{n}\in\mathcal{P}^{*}_{n}
.
We want to test if
H_{0}:\mathbb{E}(X_{1}^{n})=\theta_{n}
against the alternative hypothesis
H_{1}:\mathbb{E}(X_{1}^{n})=\theta_{n}+\frac{z_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}
. Using
eq.

237
we know that it is possible to find a rejection region
R_{n}
such that:

P(X^{n}\in R_{n}|H_{0})+P(X^{n}\not\in R_{n}|H_{1})\rightarrow 0.

However by hypothesis the Kullback-Leibler divergence is smooth and according to the Taylor expansion we know that

KL\left(p_{\theta_{n}}^{n}+\frac{z_{n}}{\sqrt{n}},p_{\theta_{n}}^{n}\right)=\frac{1}{2n}z_{n}^{\top}\mathcal{I}_{n}(\tilde{\theta}_{n})z_{n},

where
\tilde{\theta}_{n}\in[\theta_{n},\theta_{n}+\frac{z_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}]
. Moreover by definition of the total variation distance and the inequality:
\|p(\cdot)-q(\cdot)\|_{TV}\geq 1-\frac{1}{2}e^{-KL(p,q)}
(see
Lemma

12
) we know that for all rejection region
R_{n}
we have

\begin{split}&P(X^{n}\in R_{n}|H_{0})+P(X^{n}\not\in R_{n}|H_{1})\\
&\geq\frac{1}{2}e^{-nKL(p_{\theta_{n}}^{n}+\frac{z_{n}}{\sqrt{n}},p_{\theta_{n}}^{n})}\\
&\overset{}{\geq}\frac{1}{2}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{z_{n}^{\top}\mathcal{I}_{n}(\tilde{\theta}_{n})z_{n}}}.\end{split}

Therefore using (i) we obtain that
\liminf_{n}P(X^{n}\in R_{n}|H_{0})+P(X^{n}\not\in R_{n}|H_{1})>0
and note there is a contradiction. Hence we have successfully showed that the desired result holds.
∎

E.5
Proof of
Theorem

4

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of using
theorem

14
for the random functions

\tilde{g}_{n}:x_{1:n}\rightarrow g_{n}\Big(x_{1}+\mathbb{E}(X^{n}_{1})-\bar{X^{n}},\dots,x_{n}+\mathbb{E}(X^{n}_{1})-\bar{X^{n}}\Big);

which can be approximated by the following smooth functions:

\tilde{f}_{n}:x_{1:n}\rightarrow f_{n}\Big(x_{1}+\mathbb{E}(X^{n}_{1})-\bar{X^{n}},\dots,x_{n}+\mathbb{E}(X^{n}_{1})-\bar{X^{n}}\Big).

∎

E.6
Proof of
Theorem

5

Proof.

By the triangle inequality we know that:

\displaystyle\left|g_{n}(Z^{n})-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{n}(Y^{n})\mid X^{n}\right]\right|\leq~

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left[g_{n}(Y^{n})-g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}\right]\right|+\left|g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}\right]\right|.

(238)

We bound successively each terms of the right-hand side. Firstly using
theorem

1
we remark that for all
\delta>0
we have

P\left[\left|\mathbb{E}\left[g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{n}(Z^{n})\mid X^{n}\right]\right|\geq\delta\right]\rightarrow 0.

Hence by combining this with the definition of
t_{b,n}^{\beta/2}
we know that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\delta\downarrow 0}P\big(\left|g_{n}(Z^{n})-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})\mid X^{n}\right]\right|\geq t_{{\rm b},n}^{\beta/2}(X^{n})+\delta\big)\leq\beta/2.

(239)

Let
N^{n}\sim N(0,\Sigma_{n})
be a Gaussian vector with variance-covariance
\Sigma_{n}
.
Using
[
16
]
we know that there is a constant
C
that does not depend on
n
such that the following holds:

\displaystyle\sup_{t}\Big|P\left[\sqrt{n}\max_{k}\big|\bar{X^{n}_{k}}-\mathbb{E}(X_{1,k}^{n})\big|\geq t\right]-P(\max_{k}|N^{n,k}|\geq t)\Big|\leq\frac{C\log(d_{n})^{7/6}\max(\|\max_{j}|X^{n}_{1,j}|\|_{L_{4}}^{4},1)}{n^{1/6}}

(240)

Combining this with hypothesis
(H_{3})
we obtain that:

\displaystyle P\left(\big|\mathbb{E}\left[g_{n}(Y^{n}+\bar{X^{n}}-\mathbb{E}(X^{n}_{1}))-g_{n}(Y^{n})\mid X^{n}\right]\big|\geq t_{g,n}^{\beta/2}\right)

(241)

\displaystyle\leq P\left(\|\sqrt{n}[\bar{X^{n}}-\mathbb{E}(X^{n}_{1})]\|_{\infty}^{\alpha}\geq\frac{t_{g,n}^{\beta/2}}{C_{n}}\right)

(242)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\log(d_{n})^{7/6}\max(\|\max_{j}|X^{n}_{1,j}|\|_{L_{4}}^{4},1)}{n^{1/6}}+P\left(\max_{k}|N_{k}^{n}|\geq\big[\frac{t_{g,n}^{\beta/2}}{C_{n}}\big]^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\right)

(243)

\displaystyle\leq\beta/2+o(1)

(244)

Therefore for all
\delta>0
we have

\displaystyle P\left(\big|g_{n}(Z^{n})-\mathbb{E}(g_{n}(Y^{n}))\big|\leq t_{{\rm b},n}^{\beta/2}(X^{n})+t_{g,n}^{\beta/2}+\delta\right)

(245)

\displaystyle\leq P\left(\big|g_{n}(Z^{n})-\mathbb{E}(g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n}))\big|\leq t_{{\rm b},n}^{\beta/2}(X^{n})+\delta\right)

(246)

\displaystyle~+P\left(\big|\mathbb{E}(g_{n}(\tilde{Y}^{n})-g_{n}(Y^{n})\mid X^{n})\big|\leq t_{g,n}^{\beta/2}\right)

(247)

\displaystyle\leq\beta+o(1)

(248)

∎

E.7
Proof of
Example

3.6

Proof.

Define the function:
g_{n}:x_{1:n}\rightarrow\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\leq n}x_{i}\right)^{2}
. It is straightforward to check that
(g_{n})
and
(X^{n})
satisfy conditions
(H_{0})
and
(H_{1})
. Moreover for all
x
we note that:

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left(g_{n}(Y^{n}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}})-g_{n}(Y^{n})\right)\right|

\displaystyle=x^{2}.

(249)

Therefore
(g_{n})
also satisfy conditions
(H_{3})
with
C_{n}=1
and
\alpha=2
. The result is a direct consequence of
Theorem

5
.
∎

E.8
Proof of
Theorem

6

Proof.

Firstly we prove that with high-probability
\bar{X}^{n}-\mathbb{E}(X_{1}^{n})
is in
B_{d_{n}}(\gamma_{n})
. Indeed, using
Lemma

8
and Chebystchev inequality we know that there is a constant
C
such that

\displaystyle P\left[\max_{k}\left|\bar{X^{n}_{k}}-\mathbb{E}(X_{1,k}^{n})\right|\geq\gamma_{n}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left(\max_{k}\left|\bar{X^{n}_{k}}-\mathbb{E}(X_{1,k}^{n})\right|^{2}\right)}{\gamma^{2}_{n}}

(250)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\log(d_{n})^{2}\left\|\max_{k\leq k_{n}}\left|X^{n}_{1,k}\right|\right\|_{L_{2}}^{2}}{\gamma_{n}^{2}n}\rightarrow 0.

(251)

Therefore with high-probability
\tilde{\mu}:=\bar{X}^{n}-\mathbb{E}(X_{1}^{n})
is in
B_{d_{n}}(\gamma)
. Moreover, we note that:

\displaystyle P\left(\left|g_{n}(Z^{n}-\tilde{\mu})-\mathbb{E}(g_{n}(Z^{n}-\tilde{\mu})\mid X^{n})\right|\geq t^{\alpha}_{*}(X^{n})~\mid X^{n}\right)\leq\alpha.

(252)

We remark that
Z^{n}-\tilde{\mu}=\tilde{Z}^{n}
therefore according to
Theorem

4
for all
\delta>0
we have:

\displaystyle{P\left(\left|g_{n}(Y^{n})-\mathbb{E}(g_{n}(Y^{n})\right|\geq t^{\alpha}_{*}(X^{n})+\delta\right)\mid X^{n}}\leq\alpha+o_{n}(1).

(253)

Which implies that asymptotically the confidence intervals
\big[t^{\beta}_{*}(X^{n})-\delta,~t^{\alpha}_{*}(X^{n})+\delta\big]
has an asymptotic coverage of at least
1-\beta
.
∎

E.9
Proof of
Theorem

7

Proof.

Denote

\epsilon_{n}:=\sup_{\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{n}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}^{n}\in\mathcal{P}_{n}}\left(\left|f_{n}(\tilde{Z}^{n})-g_{n}(\tilde{Z}^{n})\right|\right)+\mathbb{E}\left(\left|f_{n}({Y}^{n})-g_{n}({Y}^{n})\right|\right)+\sup_{\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{n}}\max\left(R_{n,1}^{c,\nu},R_{n,2}^{c,\nu},R_{n,3}^{c,\nu}\right).

Choose
\nu\in\mathcal{Q}_{n}
, and let
X^{n}\sim\nu
if
t^{\alpha}_{n}(X^{n})
is chosen such that:

{P\left(\left|g_{n}(Z^{n}+\mathbb{E}(X^{n}_{1})-\bar{X^{n}})-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{n}(Z^{n}-\mathbb{E}(X^{n}_{1})+\bar{X^{n}})\mid X^{n}\right]\right|\geq t_{n}^{\alpha}(X^{n})\mid X^{n}\right)}\leq\alpha.

(254)

then according to
Theorem

4
and
1
for all
\delta>0
we have

P\left(\left|g_{n}(X^{n})-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{n}(X^{n})\right]\right|\geq t^{\alpha}_{n}+\delta\right)\leq\alpha+O(\epsilon_{n}).

(255)

But as
\mathbb{E}(X_{1}^{n})\in A_{n}
by definition of
t_{n}^{\alpha}(X^{n})
we know that:
t_{n}^{\alpha}(X^{n})\leq t_{\alpha}^{*,n}
. This directly implies that

{{P_{X^{n},Y^{n}\overset{i.i.d}{\sim}\nu}\left(\left|g_{n}(Y^{n})-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{n}(Y^{n})\right]\right|\geq t_{\alpha}^{*,n}(X^{n})+\delta\right)}}\leq\alpha+O(\epsilon_{n}).

(256)

As this holds for all
\nu\in A_{n}
we get the desired result.
∎

Appendix F
Proofs from
Section

4

F.1
Proof of
example

4.1

Proof.

We will use
Theorem

1
.
We note that the condition
(H_{0})
holds automatically as the functions
(g_{n})
are three-times differentiable. Therefore to we only need to verify that
(H_{1})
holds. By the chain rule we have:

\partial_{i}g_{n}(Z^{n,i,\bar{X}^{n}})=\frac{p}{\sqrt{n}}\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{j\neq i}^{n}X_{j}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bar{X^{n}}\big)^{p-1};

and

\partial_{i}^{2}g_{n}(Z^{n,i,\bar{X^{n}}})=\frac{p(p-1)\mathbb{I}(p\geq 2)}{n}\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bar{X^{n}}\big)^{p-2};

\partial_{i}^{3}g_{n}(Z^{n,i,x})=\frac{p(p-1)(p-2)\mathbb{I}(p\geq 3)}{\sqrt{n}^{3}}\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\big)^{p-3}.

Moreover, according to the Rosenthal inequality for martingales
[
30
]
, there is a constant
C
such that

\begin{split}&\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\right\|_{12p}\leq C\|X_{1}\|_{12p}\end{split}

This implies that:
R_{n,1}=O(n^{-1/2}),\quad R_{n,2}=O(n),\quad\&\quad R_{n,3}=O(n^{-3/2}).

Theorem

1
concludes the proof.
∎

F.2
Proof of
Example

4.2

Proof.

We define
g_{n}(X):=\sqrt{n}\prod_{l=1}^{n}(1+\frac{X_{l}}{{n}}).
Condition
(H_{0})
holds automatically as
g_{n}
is smooth. To obtain the desired result we only need to verify that
(H_{1})
holds.
By the chain rule we have:

\partial_{i}g_{n}(Z^{n,i,\bar{X^{n}}})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\prod_{l\neq i}(1+\frac{Z^{n}_{l}}{{n}})\times(1+\frac{\bar{X^{n}}}{n});\quad\rm{and}\quad\partial_{i}^{2}g_{n}(Z^{n,i})=0.

Therefore we note that for condition
(H_{1})
to hold we only need to upper bound
\partial_{i}g_{n}(Z^{n,i,\bar{X^{n}}})
. Using the fact that the observations
(X_{i})
are assumed to be bounded we know that there is a constant
C
such that

\begin{split}&\big\|\prod_{l\neq i}\Big(1+\frac{Z^{n}_{l}}{{n}}\Big)\big\|_{L_{12}}^{12}\\
&\overset{(a)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\Big[\prod_{l\neq i}\mathbb{E}\Big((1+\frac{Z^{n}_{l}}{{n}})^{12}\Big|X\Big)\Big]\\
&\overset{(b)}{\leq}\prod_{l\neq i}(1+\frac{\max(C,1)}{n})\sim e^{\max(C,1)}\end{split}

where to get (a) we exploited the conditional independence of the observations
(Z^{n}_{i})
and to obtain (b) we used the fact that the observations
(X_{i})
are assumed to be bounded. This implies that
(H_{1})
holds.
∎

F.3
Proof of
Example

4.3

Proof.

We want to used
Theorem

1
and
1
. Let
(\beta_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence satisfying: (i)
\beta_{n}\rightarrow\infty
and (ii)
\beta_{n}=o(n^{1/4})

We define
(f_{n}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R})
as the following sequence of functions:

f_{n}(x_{1:n}):=\frac{1}{\beta_{n}}\log\left(1+e^{\beta_{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\leq n}x_{i}}\right).

We note that the functions
(f_{n})
are three times differentiable functions and that the following holds:

\left\|f_{n}(Z^{n})-g_{n}(Z^{n})\right\|\leq\frac{\log(2)}{\beta_{n}}\quad\rm{and}\quad\left\|f_{n}(Z^{n})-g_{n}(Z^{n})\right\|\leq\frac{\log(2)}{\beta_{n}}.

Therefore the functions
(f_{n})
and
(g_{n})
satisfy conditions
(H_{0})
. Moreover by the chain rule we have:

\partial_{i}f_{n}(x_{1:n})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},\quad\partial^{2}_{i}f_{n}(x_{1:n})=\frac{\beta_{n}}{{n}},\quad\partial^{3}_{i}f_{n}(x_{1:n})=\frac{\beta_{n}^{2}}{{n}^{3/2}}.

This implies that condition
(H_{1})
also holds as
R_{n,1}=O(n^{-1/2}),\quad R_{n,2}=o(n^{-1/2}),\quad\&\quad R_{n,3}=0(n^{-1}).

1
and
Theorem

1
conclude the proof.

∎

F.4
Proof of
Example

4.4

Proof.

For ease of notations for all element
\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
we denote by
(\mathbf{z}_{1},\mathbf{z}_{2})
its coordinates. Moreover we remark that the i.i.d random variables
(X^{n}_{i,j})
are indexed by
\llbracket n\rrbracket^{2}
rather than
\llbracket n^{2}\rrbracket.
As there is a one-to-one mapping between those two sets, we note that the random variables
(X^{n}_{i,j})
could be indexed by
\llbracket n^{2}\rrbracket.
Therefore
Theorem

1
applies. However for ease of notations we keep the original indexing.

We note that
(g_{n})
is three times differentiable so
(H_{0})
holds. Moreover by the chain rule we have:

\partial_{\mathbf{z}}g_{n}(ZZ^{n,\mathbf{z},\overline{X^{n}}}):=\frac{1}{n\sqrt{n}}\frac{\sum_{m\in\{-1,1\}^{n}}m_{\mathbf{z}_{1}}m_{\mathbf{z}_{2}}e^{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}m^{\top}ZZ^{n,\mathbf{z},\overline{X^{n}}}m}}{\sum_{m\in\{-1,1\}^{n}}e^{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}m^{\top}ZZ^{n,\mathbf{z},\overline{X^{n}}}m}}.

(257)

This implies that

\big\|\partial_{\mathbf{z}}g_{n}(ZZ^{n,\mathbf{z},\overline{X^{n}}})\big\|_{L_{12}}\leq\frac{1}{n\sqrt{n}}.

(258)

Using the chain rule we have:

\partial^{2}_{\mathbf{z}}g_{n}(ZZ^{n,\mathbf{z},\overline{X^{n}}}):=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\frac{\sum_{m\in\{-1,1\}^{n}}(m_{\mathbf{z}_{1}}m_{\mathbf{z}_{2}})^{2}e^{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}m^{\top}ZZ^{n,\mathbf{z},\overline{X^{n}}}m}}{\sum_{m\in\{-1,1\}^{n}}e^{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}m^{\top}ZZ^{n,\mathbf{z},\overline{X^{n}}}m}}-\frac{1}{n^{2}}\Big[\frac{\sum_{m\in\{-1,1\}^{n}}m_{\mathbf{z}_{1}}m_{\mathbf{z}_{2}}e^{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}m^{\top}ZZ^{n,\mathbf{z},\overline{X^{n}}}m}}{\sum_{m\in\{-1,1\}^{n}}e^{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}m^{\top}ZZ^{n,\mathbf{z},\overline{X^{n}}}m}}\Big]^{2}.

(259)

This implies that

\big\|\partial^{2}_{\mathbf{z}}g_{n}(ZZ^{n,\mathbf{z},\overline{X^{n}}})\big\|_{L_{12}}\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}}.

(260)

Finally by a last use of the chain rule we have

\begin{split}\partial^{3}_{\mathbf{z}}g_{n}(ZZ^{n,\mathbf{z},x}):=&\frac{1}{n^{5/2}}\frac{\sum_{m\in\{-1,1\}^{n}}m_{\mathbf{z}_{1}}m_{\mathbf{z}_{2}}e^{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}m^{\top}ZZ^{n,\mathbf{z},\overline{X^{n}}}m}}{\sum_{m\in\{-1,1\}^{n}}e^{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}m^{\top}ZZ^{n,\mathbf{z},\overline{X^{n}}}m}}\Big[1-\Big[\frac{\sum_{m\in\{-1,1\}^{n}}m_{\mathbf{z}_{1}}m_{\mathbf{z}_{2}}e^{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}m^{\top}ZZ^{n,\mathbf{z},\overline{X^{n}}}m}}{\sum_{m\in\{-1,1\}^{n}}e^{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}m^{\top}ZZ^{n,\mathbf{z},\overline{X^{n}}}m}}\Big]^{2}\Big]\end{split}

This implies that

\left\|\sup_{x\in[Z_{\mathbf{z}}^{n},\tilde{X}^{n}]\cup[\tilde{Y}_{\mathbf{z}}^{n},\tilde{X}^{n}]}\left|\partial^{3}_{\mathbf{z}}g_{n}(ZZ^{n,\mathbf{z},x})\right|\right\|_{L_{12}}\leq\frac{1}{n^{5/2}}.

(261)

This implies that the desired result holds. The same can be proved for the centered bootstrap
(\tilde{Z}^{n}_{\mathbf{z}})
and
(Y_{\mathbf{z}}^{n})
.
∎

Appendix G
Proofs from
Section

5

G.1
Proof of
3

Proof.

We note that we can suppose without loss of generality that
\mathbb{E}\left(X_{1}^{n}\right)=0
. Let
(\beta_{n})
be a sequence of reals satisfying:
(i)

\beta_{n}\rightarrow\infty
and (ii)
\beta_{n}=o\big(\frac{n^{1/8}}{\sqrt{\log(p_{n})}}\big)
.

To get the desired result we use
Theorem

13
. In this goal we define
(f_{n})
to be the following sequence of function:

f_{n}(x_{1:n}):=\frac{\log\Big(\sum_{l\leq p_{n}}e^{\beta_{n}\log(p_{n})\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\leq n}x^{\prime}_{i,l}}\Big)}{\beta_{n}\log(p_{n})}.

We remark that the functions
(f_{n})
are three times differentiable, and that they satisfy:

\begin{split}&\Big\|f_{n}(\tilde{Z}^{n})-g_{n}(\tilde{Z}^{n})\Big\|_{L_{1}}\leq\frac{1}{\beta_{n}}\\
&\Big\|f_{n}(Y^{n})-g_{n}(Y^{n})\Big\|_{L_{1}}\leq\frac{1}{\beta_{n}}.\end{split}

We prove that the conditions of
Theorem

13
hold. Using
section

G.1
we know that hypothesis
(H_{0})
is holding; and we only need to prove that
(H_{1}^{*})
also holds. For ease of notations we write:

\omega_{k}(x_{1:n}):=\frac{e^{\beta\log(p_{n})\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\leq n}x_{i,k}}}{\sum_{l\leq p_{n}}e^{\beta\log(p_{n})\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\leq n}x_{i,l}}}.

By the chain rule we remark that for all
k_{1}\leq p_{n}
and all
x_{1:n}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{n}}
we have

\begin{split}\Big|\partial_{i,k_{1}}f_{n}(x_{1:n})\Big|&\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\omega_{k_{1}}(x^{\prime}_{1:n})\end{split}

As
\sum_{k\leq p_{n}}\omega_{k}(x_{1:n})=1
we obtain for all
i\leq n
that
